,
e_{{\mathbf{F}}}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{t}-\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}}^{-1}\mathbf{F}_{t}(\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{C}}^{-1})^{\top}\|_{2}^{2}
, and
e_{{\mathbf{S}}}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\widehat{\mathbf{S}}_{t}-\mathbf{S}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
from our method and
\alpha
-PCA. All values are multiplied by 100. Data are generated from model (
2.1
),
{\mathbf{Y}}_{t}={\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{F}}_{t}{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{E}}_{t}
, where
{\mathbf{F}}_{t}
and
{\mathbf{E}}_{t}
follow the AR(1) processes in Equation (
5.2
) with
(k,r)=(3,3)
, and entries of
{\mathbf{R}}
and
{\mathbf{C}}
are drawn independently from
\mathcal{U}(-1,1)
. We consider four dimension settings,
(p,q)\in\{(50,50),(50,100),(100,50),(100,100)\}
, four sample size settings,
T\in\{0.5pq,1.0pq,1.5pq,2.0pq\}
, and two levels of temporal dependence of
{\mathbf{E}}_{t}
,
\psi\in\{0.1,0.5\}
. Results are based on 200 repetitions, with
\alpha=0
,
s_{1}=s_{2}=5
, and even partitions.

5.1.2
Asymptotic normality of loading matrix estimators

We next examine the asymptotic normality of
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{RH_{R}}
established in Theorem
4
. Specifically, we focus on the first row
\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}^{1}-{\mathbf{R}}^{1}{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathbf{R}}\in\mathbb{R}^{1\times k}
. Without loss of generality, we generate
{\mathbf{F}}_{t}
and
{\mathbf{E}}_{t}
with independent rows and columns as

\displaystyle{\mathbf{F}}_{t}\sim\mathcal{MN}_{3\times 3}\left(3\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}\right),\quad{\mathbf{E}}_{t}\sim\mathcal{MN}_{p\times q}\left(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I},\mathbf{I}\right).

(5.4)

Under the conditions
{\sqrt{\overline{m}T}}/{p}=\mathbf{o}(1)
and
{\overline{m}}/{\underline{m}}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, Theorem
4
implies that, as
\underline{m},p,T\to\infty
,

\sqrt{\overline{m}T}\left(\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}^{1}-{\mathbf{R}}^{1}{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathbf{R}}\right)^{\top}\overset{\mathcal{D}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{N}\left(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1}}\right).

We estimate the asymptotic covariance
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1}}
by
\widehat{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1}}
defined in Theorem
5
, yielding

\widehat{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1}}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\overline{m}T}(\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}^{1}-{\mathbf{R}}^{1}{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathbf{R}})^{\top}\overset{\mathcal{D}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{N}\left(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{I}\right).

We first consider the special case
s_{1}=1
(i.e., single-machine estimation). Figure B2 in Appendix B displays the empirical distribution of the first element of
\widehat{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1}}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\overline{m}T}(\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}^{1}-{\mathbf{R}}^{1}{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathbf{R}})^{\top}
for various sample sizes. As expected, the distribution approaches the standard normal distribution as
T
increases, consistent with
8
. We next consider the case
s_{1}=3
. Figure
2
exhibits similar convergence behavior, where the empirical distribution of its first element also approaches the standard normal distribution as
T
grows, confirming our theoretical results.

Figure 2
:
Density histograms of the first element of
\widehat{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{1}}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\overline{m}T}(\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}^{1}-{\mathbf{R}}^{1}{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathbf{R}})^{\top}
. Red curves denote the standard normal distribution. Data are generated from model (
2.1
),
{\mathbf{Y}}_{t}={\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{F}}_{t}{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{E}}_{t}
, where
{\mathbf{F}}_{t}
and
{\mathbf{E}}_{t}
follow the matrix normal distributions in Equation (
5.4
) with
(k,r)=(3,3)
and
(p,q)=(200,200)
, and entries of
{\mathbf{R}}
and
{\mathbf{C}}
are drawn independently from
\mathcal{U}(-1,1)
. We consider four sample size settings,
T\in\{0.5pq,1.0pq,1.5pq,2.0pq\}
. Results are based on 1000 repetitions, with
\alpha=0
,
s_{1}=s_{2}=3
, and even partitions.

5.1.3
Computational efficiency

We evaluated the convergence rates and asymptotic normality of our estimators, showing accuracy comparable to that of
\alpha
-PCA. To further illustrate the computational savings of our method, we report the average runtime for estimating loading matrices under two scenarios. In setting I, we fix
p=q=100
and increase
T
from 100 to 10000; In setting II, we fix
T=100
and increase
p=q
from 50 to 1000. Figure
3
demonstrates the substantial computational gains of our method relative to
\alpha
-PCA, consistent with the discussion in Section
2.3
.

Figure 3
:
Average runtime (log s) for estimating loading matrices using our method and
\alpha
-PCA. Data are generated from model (
2.1
),
{\mathbf{Y}}_{t}={\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{F}}_{t}{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{E}}_{t}
, where
{\mathbf{F}}_{t}
and
{\mathbf{E}}_{t}
follow the AR(1) processes in Equation (
5.2
) with
(k,r)=(3,3)
and
\psi=0.1
, and entries of
{\mathbf{R}}
and
{\mathbf{C}}
are drawn independently from
\mathcal{U}(-1,1)
. Two settings are considered: In setting I, we fix
p=q=100
and increase
T
from 100 to 10,000; In setting II, we fix
T=100
and increase
p=q
from 50 to 1,000. Results are based on 200 repetitions, with
\alpha=0
,
s_{1}=s_{2}=5
, and even partitions.

5.1.4
Accuracy of estimators in the unit-root nonstationary case

Lastly, we assess the estimation accuracy of our method for unit-root nonstationary time series. The observations are still generated from model (
2.1
),
{\mathbf{Y}}_{t}={\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{F}}_{t}{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}+{\mathbf{E}}_{t}
, where
{\mathbf{F}}_{t}
is generated following
31
and
{\mathbf{E}}_{t}
is generated as described in Section
5.1.1
. Specifically, we generate
{\mathbf{F}}_{t}
and
{\mathbf{E}}_{t}
as,

\text{vec}({\mathbf{F}}_{t})=\text{vec}({\mathbf{F}}_{t-1})+{\mathbf{w}}_{t},\quad{\mathbf{w}}_{t}=0.3{\mathbf{w}}_{t-1}+\bm{\xi}_{t},

(5.5)

and

\text{vec}({\mathbf{E}}_{t})=0.1\text{vec}({\mathbf{E}}_{t-1})+\bm{\xi}^{\prime}_{t},

(5.6)

with initial values
{\mathbf{F}}_{0}=\bm{0}
,
{\mathbf{w}}_{0}=\bm{0}
, and
{\mathbf{E}}_{0}=\bm{0}
. The noise vectors
\bm{\xi}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{kr}
and
\bm{\xi}^{\prime}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{pq}
are i.i.d., with each element drawn from the standard normal distribution.

We evaluate the accuracy of
\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}
,
\widehat{{\mathbf{C}}}
,
\widehat{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}
, and
\widehat{{\mathbf{S}}}_{t}
. Table
5.4
reports means and standard deviations, based on 200 repetitions, of
e_{{\mathbf{R}}}=\frac{1}{p}\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{RH_{R}}\|_{2}^{2}
,
e_{{\mathbf{C}}}=\frac{1}{q}\|\widehat{\mathbf{C}}-\mathbf{CH_{C}}\|_{2}^{2}
,
e_{{\mathbf{F}}}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\widehat{\mathbf{F}}_{t}-\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}}^{-1}\mathbf{F}_{t}(\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{C}}^{-1})^{\top}\|_{2}^{2}
, and
e_{{\mathbf{S}}}=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\|\widehat{\mathbf{S}}_{t}-\mathbf{S}_{t}\|_{2}^{2}
. Across all settings, our method achieves substantially higher accuracy than in the stationary case, consistent with our theoretical results in Section
4
.

5.2
Real data applications

In this section, we apply our method to the Fama–French Stock Return Data and the OECD CPI data to assess its predictive performance.

5.2.1
Example 1: Fama–French Stock Return Data

We first apply our method to the Fama–French stock return dataset analyzed in
39
. The dataset contains monthly returns for 100 portfolios formed by intersecting 10 size levels with 10 book-to-market levels. The sample spans July 1926 to October 2025, covering 1,192 months. Details are available at
http://mba.tuck.dartmouth.edu/pages/faculty/ken.french/data_library.html
.

To compare our method with
\alpha
-PCA, we take the earliest 85% of the observations as a training set to estimate the loading matrices, and the remaining 15% as a test set to evaluate their performance. We define out-of-sample
R^{2}
on the test set
{\mathbf{Y}}_{t}^{\text{test}}
of size
N
as:

\text{out-of-sample }R^{2}=1-\frac{\sum_{t=1}^{N}\left\|{\mathbf{Y}}_{t}^{\text{test}}-\widehat{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{t}^{\text{test}}\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}}{\sum_{t=1}^{N}\left\|{\mathbf{Y}}_{t}^{\text{test}}-\overline{{\mathbf{Y}}}^{\text{test}}\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}},

(5.7)

where
\widehat{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{t}^{\text{test}}=\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}^{\top}\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}{\mathbf{Y}}_{t}^{\text{test}}\widehat{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\top}\widehat{{\mathbf{C}}}
and
\overline{{\mathbf{Y}}}^{\text{test}}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{t=1}^{N}{\mathbf{Y}}_{t}^{\text{test}}
, with
\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}
and
\widehat{{\mathbf{C}}}
estimated from the training set. The denominator is the total sum of squares (TSS), which measures the deviation of
{\mathbf{Y}}_{t}^{\text{test}}
from its sample mean
\overline{{\mathbf{Y}}}^{\text{test}}
. The numerator is the residual sum of squares (RSS), measuring the deviation of
{\mathbf{Y}}_{t}^{\text{test}}
from its estimator
\widehat{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{t}^{\text{test}}
constructed by the training set.

Table
5.5
reports the training time and out-of-sample
R^{2}
of our method and
\alpha
-PCA on the stock return dataset under various assumed numbers of factors, along with
p
-values from the Diebold–Mariano test
(
10
)
used to compare their predictive performance on the RSS. The null hypothesis of interest is that the two methods yield equal forecast errors,
\mathbb{E}[\|{\mathbf{Y}}_{t}^{\text{test}}-\widehat{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{t}^{\text{test,ours}}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}-\|{\mathbf{Y}}_{t}^{\text{test}}-\widehat{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{t}^{\text{test},\alpha}\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}]=0
, where
\widehat{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{t}^{\text{test,ours}}
and
\widehat{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{t}^{\text{test},\alpha}
denote our estimator and
\alpha
-PCA estimator, against the alternative that our method produces a smaller RSS. The eigenvalue ratio-based method suggests that
(k,r)=(4,1)
. The numbers of computing nodes,
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
, are treated as tuning parameters and selected from 1 to 5 to maximize out-of-sample
R^{2}
, with their chosen values reported in the table. The distributed data allocation follows the strategy in Section
2.5
. The observations, which are already sorted, are allocated sequentially and evenly to the nodes.

Across all assumed factor numbers, our method consistently achieves higher out-of-sample
R^{2}
, with all
p
-values below 0.05, indicating improved forecastability from our distributed estimation procedure. Moreover, despite the low dimensionality of the dataset, our method still exhibits substantial reductions in training time, consistent with our theoretical analysis and simulation results. This finding suggests that our framework achieves significant computational savings in high-dimensional settings.

This table reports the training time (s) and out-of-sample
R^{2}
of our method and
\alpha
-PCA on the stock return dataset under various assumed numbers of factors, along with
p
-values based on the Diebold–Mariano test. The dataset contains monthly returns for
10\times 10
portfolios, covering 1,192 months. We take the earliest 85% of the observations as a training set, and the remaining 15% as a test set. The out-of-sample
R^{2}
is computed according to Equation (
5.7
), with tuning parameters
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
selected to maximize out-of-sample
R^{2}
. In our method, we set
s_{1},s_{2}\in\{1,2,...,5\}
with even partitions, and in
\alpha
-PCA we use
\alpha=0
. The eigenvalue ratio-based method suggests that
(k,r)=(4,1)
.

5.2.2
Example 2: OECD CPI Data

In this example, we analyze a multinational macroeconomic dataset collected from OECD, similar to those used in
8
and
40
. The dataset contains monthly Consumer Price Index (CPI) statistics from 1914 to 2025, covering 27 expenditure items for 52 countries and international organizations over 1343 months. The lists of countries and expenditure items are provided in Table B3 in Appendix B. Further details are available at
https://data-explorer.oecd.org/vis?df[ds]=dsDisseminateFinalDMZ&df[id]=DSD_PRICES%40DF_PRICES_ALL&df[ag]=OECD.SDD.TPS
.

We compare the predictive performance of our method with
\alpha
-PCA using the earliest 95% of the observations used for training and the remaining 5% for testing. Table
5.6
reports the training time and out-of-sample
R^{2}
of both methods on the CPI dataset under various assumed numbers of factors, along with
p
-values from the Diebold–Mariano test
(
10
)
used to compare their predictive performance on the RSS. The eigenvalue ratio-based method suggests that
(k,r)=(3,2)
. The numbers of computing nodes,
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
, are treated as tuning parameters and selected from 1 to 10 to maximize out-of-sample
R^{2}
, with their chosen values reported in the table. The distributed data allocation also follows the strategy in Section
2.5
. The observations are first sorted by GDP rankings along the nation dimension (World Bank, December 16, 2024) and by variance along the item dimension in decreasing order, and are then sequentially and evenly allocated to the nodes. Across all assumed factor numbers, our method consistently achieves shorter training times and higher out-of-sample
R^{2}
, with all
p
-values below 0.05, indicating both improved computational efficiency and enhanced forecastability. Moreover, the effect of
(s_{1},s_{2})
on training times is clearly observed in the table and aligns well with the discussion in Section
2.3
.

), with tuning parameters

. The eigenvalue ratio-based method suggests that

.

Next, we analyze connections among countries and among expenditure items by clustering the loading matrix estimators. Guided by the results in Table
5.6
, we set
(k,r)=(3,2)
and
(s_{1},s_{2})=(4,9)
for the subsequent analysis, and estimate the model using the full dataset. For better illustration, the estimators are normalized and rotated using the VARIMAX criterion
(
26
)
. Clustering our rotated estimators
\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{\text{ours,rot}}
and
\widehat{{\mathbf{C}}}_{\text{ours,rot}}
, together with the
\alpha
-PCA estimators
\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{\alpha\text{,rot}}
and
\widehat{{\mathbf{C}}}_{\alpha\text{,rot}}
), yields groupings of countries and expenditure items, respectively. The rotated estimators and the corresponding clusters are reported in Table B1 and Table B2 in Appendix B, and Table
5.7
summarizes the mean loadings within each cluster for our rotated estimators.

From the rotated estimator
\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{\text{ours,rot}}
, we can classify the countries into four groups: Group 1 (TUR), Group 2 (DEU, JPN, GBR, FRA, KOR, CHE, SWE, IRL, …), Group 3 (G20, OECD, EU27, G7, CHN, IND, BRA, RUS, …), and Group 4 (USA, ITA, CAN, MEX, ESP, NLD, POL, BEL, …). Table B1 and Table
5.7
show that Group 1, Turkey, loads most heavily on Column 1, reflecting its distinct high inflation among large economies. Group 2, mainly consisting of developed economies with advanced manufacturing sectors, exhibits negative loadings on Columns 2 and 3. Group 3, which comprises large developing economies, loads primarily on column 3. Finally, Group 4, comprising countries with strong economic ties to the United States but less developed manufacturing sectors, loads negatively on Column 2 and positively on Column 3. Overall, these clusters reflect similarities in domestic economic patterns. Moreover, the
\alpha
-PCA estimators produce groupings for larger economies that are broadly consistent with our clusters, while some differences arise for smaller economies, suggesting the differences in how the two methods capture localized heterogeneity. An analogous analysis applies to the groupings of expenditure items and is therefore omitted.

This table reports the mean loadings by cluster for our rotated estimators
\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{\text{ours,rot}}
and
\widehat{{\mathbf{C}}}_{\text{ours,rot}}

, with

and even partitions in our method, and

Concluding Remarks

We proposed a distributed estimation procedure for modeling both stationary and unit-root nonstationary high-dimensional matrix-valued time series. The method preserved the intrinsic matrix structure and introduced a novel partitioning regime, which enabled accurate and computationally efficient extraction of latent factors while maintaining the overall common structure and respecting inter-group heterogeneity. We established statistical guarantees for the resulting estimators, including consistency and asymptotic normality. Numerical studies further demonstrated that the proposed method achieved substantial computational gains without sacrificing reliable finite-sample performance. These properties positioned the proposed procedure as a compelling alternative for factor modeling in high-dimensional stationary and nonstationary time series in the big data era.

Appendix A: Proof of Theorems

In this section, we establish the convergence and asymptotic normality of the proposed estimators for both stationary and unit-root nonstationary time series.

A.1 Propositions and lemmas for the stationary case

We first present some propositions and lemmas for the stationary case. These results will be used to prove Theorems 1-5. We begin by establishing asymptotic properties of the local estimators and the associated auxiliary matrices. Following Lemma B.1 in
40
, we directly have Proposition
1
.

Proposition 1
.

Under Assumptions 1-6(a) with known
k
and
r
, for any
i\in[s_{1}]
and
j\in[s_{2}]
, we have

\frac{1}{p}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}-\mathbf{RH}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{1}{m_{i}T}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right),\quad\text{as }m_{i},p,T\to\infty,

and

\frac{1}{q}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}-\mathbf{CH}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{j}}\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{1}{n_{j}T}+\frac{1}{q^{2}}\right),\quad\text{as }n_{j},q,T\to\infty.

For any
l_{1}\in[p]
and
l_{2}\in[q]
, we have

\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}^{l_{1}}-\mathbf{R}^{l_{1}}\mathbf{H}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{1}{m_{i}T}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right),\quad\text{as }m_{i},p,T\to\infty,

and

\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{l_{2}}-\mathbf{C}^{l_{2}}\mathbf{H}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{j}}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{1}{n_{j}T}+\frac{1}{q^{2}}\right),\quad\text{as }n_{j},q,T\to\infty.

By applying Propositions 1-3 and Theorem 2 in
8
, we directly obtain Propositions
2
-
3
.

Proposition 2
.

Under Assumptions 1-6(a) with known
k
and
r
, for any
i\in[s_{1}]
and
j\in[s_{2}]
, we have
\|{\mathbf{H}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
, and
\|{\mathbf{H}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{j}}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
.

Proposition 3
.

Under Assumptions 1-6(a) with known
k
and
r
, for any
i\in[s_{1}]
and
j\in[s_{2}]
, we have

(a)

\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}},\quad\text{as }m_{i},p,T\to\infty,\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}},\quad\text{as }n_{j},q,T\to\infty,

with
\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
,
\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
,
\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
, and
\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{-1}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
.

(b)

\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{R}}{p}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}},\quad\text{as }m_{i},p,T\to\infty,\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{C}}{q}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}},\quad\text{as }n_{j},q,T\to\infty.

(c)

\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1},\quad\text{as }m_{i},p,T\to\infty,\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{-1},\quad\text{as }n_{j},q,T\to\infty.

(d)

When
{\sqrt{m_{i}T}}/{p}=\mathbf{o}(1)
, for any
l_{1}\in[p]
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{m_{i}T}\left(\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{i}^{l_{1}}-{\mathbf{R}}^{l_{1}}{\mathbf{H}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}\right)^{\top}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1}\frac{\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}}}{p}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{E}}}_{it}^{l_{1}})^{\top}+\mathbf{o}_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathcal{D}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{N}\left(\mathbf{0},\bm{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i,{l_{1}}}}\right),\quad\text{as }m_{i},p,T\to\infty.

When
{\sqrt{n_{j}T}}/{q}=\mathbf{o}(1)
, for any
l_{2}\in[q]
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n_{j}T}\left(\widehat{{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{l_{2}}-{\mathbf{C}}^{l_{2}}{\mathbf{H}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{j}}\right)^{\top}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{-1}\frac{\widehat{{\mathbf{C}}}_{j}^{\top}{\mathbf{C}}}{q}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{j}T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{\top}[(\widetilde{\bm{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{jt}^{\top})^{l_{2}}]^{\top}+\mathbf{o}_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathcal{D}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{N}\left(\mathbf{0},\bm{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{j,{l_{2}}}}\right),\quad\text{as }n_{j},q,T\to\infty.

The covariance matrices

\bm{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i,{l_{1}}}}={\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}^{-1}{\mathbf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}\left(\mathbf{\Phi}_{R_{i},{l_{1}}}^{1,1}+\alpha\mathbf{\Phi}_{R_{i},{l_{1}}}^{1,2}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}^{\top}+\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\mathbf{\Phi}_{R_{i},{l_{1}}}^{2,1}+\alpha^{2}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\mathbf{\Phi}_{R_{i},{l_{1}}}^{2,2}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}^{\top}\right){\mathbf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}^{\top}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}^{-1},

and

\bm{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{j,{l_{2}}}}={\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{j}}^{-1}{\mathbf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{j}}\left(\mathbf{\Phi}_{C_{j},{l_{2}}}^{1,1}+\alpha\mathbf{\Phi}_{C_{j},{l_{2}}}^{1,2}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}+\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}^{\top}\mathbf{\Phi}_{C_{j},{l_{2}}}^{2,1}+\alpha^{2}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}^{\top}\mathbf{\Phi}_{C_{j},{l_{2}}}^{2,2}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\right){\mathbf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{j}}^{\top}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{j}}^{-1},

where

\mathbf{\Phi}_{R_{i},{l_{1}}}^{1,1}=\underset{q,T\rightarrow\infty}{\operatorname{plim}}\frac{1}{m_{i}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\top}({{\mathbf{E}}}_{it}^{l_{1}})^{\top}{{\mathbf{E}}}_{is}^{l_{1}}\mathbf{C}_{i}\mathbf{F}_{s}^{\top}\right],

\mathbf{\Phi}_{R_{i},{l_{1}}}^{1,2}=(\mathbf{\Phi}_{R_{i},{l_{1}}}^{2,1})^{\top}=\underset{q,T\rightarrow\infty}{\operatorname{plim}}\frac{1}{m_{i}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{F}_{t}\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\top}({{\mathbf{E}}}_{it}^{l_{1}})^{\top}{{\mathbf{E}}}_{is}^{l_{1}}\mathbf{C}_{i}\right],

and

\mathbf{\Phi}_{R_{i},{l_{1}}}^{2,2}=\underset{q,T\rightarrow\infty}{\operatorname{plim}}\frac{1}{m_{i}T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\top}({{\mathbf{E}}}_{it}^{l_{1}})^{\top}{{\mathbf{E}}}_{is}^{l_{1}}\mathbf{C}_{i}\right],

while
\mathbf{\Phi}_{C_{j},{l_{2}}}^{1,1}
,
\mathbf{\Phi}_{C_{j},{l_{2}}}^{1,2}
,
\mathbf{\Phi}_{C_{j},{l_{2}}}^{2,1}
, and
\mathbf{\Phi}_{C_{j},{l_{2}}}^{2,2}
are defined similarly.

Here the matrices
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}=\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{1/2}\Psi_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FC}_{i}}^{-1/2}
and
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}=\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{1/2}\Psi_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FR}_{j}}^{-1/2}
, where
\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}
and
\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}
are diagonal matrices of the eigenvalues (ordered decreasingly) of
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FC}_{i}}^{1/2}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FC}_{i}}^{1/2}
and
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FR}_{j}}^{1/2}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FR}_{j}}^{1/2}
, respectively, with the corresponding eigenvector matrices
\Psi_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}
and
\Psi_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}
satisfying
\Psi_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\Psi_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}=\mathbf{I}
and
\Psi_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{\top}\Psi_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}=\mathbf{I}
, along with
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FC}_{i}}=\frac{1}{m_{i}}\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{t}{\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{i}}\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{t}^{\top}]
and
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FR}_{j}}=\frac{1}{n_{j}}\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{t}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{j}}\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{t}]
.

We next derive asymptotic properties of the global estimators and the associated auxiliary matrices.

Proposition 4
.

Under Assumptions 1-6(a) with known
k
and
r
, as
\underline{m},\underline{n},T\to\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{V_{R}},\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{V_{C}},

with
\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
,
\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}}^{-1}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
,
\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
, and
\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}}^{-1}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
. Here the matrices
\mathbf{V_{R}}
and
\mathbf{V_{C}}
are diagonal matrices of eigenvalues (ordered decreasingly) of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{1/2}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{R}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{1/2}
and
\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{C}}}^{1/2}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{C}}}^{1/2}
, respectively, with
\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}=\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1}(\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1})^{\top}
and
\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{C}}}=\frac{1}{s_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{2}}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{-1})^{\top}
.

Proof.

We only prove the conclusion for
\widehat{{\mathbf{V}}}_{\mathbf{R}}
, since the proof of results for
\widehat{{\mathbf{V}}}_{\mathbf{C}}
is similar. According to Equation (2.6) and
\frac{1}{p}\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}^{\top}\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}={\mathbf{I}}
, we have

\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}}=\frac{1}{p}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{R}}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}=\frac{1}{p}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\left(\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}^{\top}\right)\widehat{\mathbf{R}}.

(A1)

Writing
\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{i}
as
\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{i}=\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{i}-{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{H}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}+{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{H}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}})(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}})^{\top}+\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}})^{\top}

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}})\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}^{\top}+\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}^{\top}.

By Propositions
1
-
2
and Assumption 3, we have

\left\|\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}})(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}})^{\top}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\underline{m}T}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right),

\left\|\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}})^{\top}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\left(\frac{1}{\underline{m}T}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{1/2}\right),

and

\left\|\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}})\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\left(\frac{1}{\underline{m}T}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{1/2}\right).

In other words,

\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{R}}-\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\left(\frac{1}{\underline{m}T}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{1/2}\right).

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}^{\top}-\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1}(\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1})^{\top}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}-\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1}(\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1})^{\top}\right\|_{2}\frac{1}{p}\left\|\mathbf{R}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{o}_{p}\left(1\right).

The conclusion follows from Assumption 3 and Proposition
3
(c), where
\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1}
, and thus
\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1}(\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1})^{\top}
, as
m_{i},p,T\to\infty
. Together, we have

\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{R}}-\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1}(\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1})^{\top}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{R}}-\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{R}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\right\|_{2}=\mathbf{o}_{p}\left(1\right).

By the inequality that for the
j
-th largest eigenvalue of any square matrices
{\mathbf{A}}
and
\widehat{{\mathbf{A}}}
,
|\lambda_{j}(\widehat{\mathbf{A}})-\lambda_{j}(\mathbf{A})|\leq\|\widehat{\mathbf{A}}-\mathbf{A}\|_{2}
, as
\underline{m},p,T\to\infty
, we have

\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{V_{R}},

where the eigenvalues of
\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{R}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}\mathbf{R}^{\top}
and
\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}
are the same,
\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\to\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{R}}
by Assumption 3, and the eigenvalues of
\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{R}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}
and
\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{1/2}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{R}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{1/2}
are also the same since
\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{R}}
and
\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}
are both positive definite. Furthermore, the top
k
eigenvalues of
\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{R}}
are bounded away from zero and infinity, and therefore we have
\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
and
\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}}^{-1}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
.
∎

Lemma 1
.

Under Assumptions 1-6(a) with known
k
and
r
, we have
\|{\mathbf{H}}_{{\mathbf{R}}}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
and
\|{\mathbf{H}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
.

Proof.

We only prove the result for
\|{\mathbf{H}}_{{\mathbf{R}}}\|_{2}
, since the proof for
\|{\mathbf{H}}_{{\mathbf{C}}}\|_{2}
is similar.
Recall that
{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathbf{R}}=\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}{\mathbf{H}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}{\mathbf{H}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}\widehat{{\mathbf{V}}}_{{\mathbf{R}}}^{-1}
. The conclusion follows directly from Assumption 3, Propositions
2
and
4
.
∎

Lemma 2
.

Under Assumptions 1-6(a) with known
k
and
r
, for any
i\in[p]
, as
\underline{m},p,T\to\infty
, we have

(a)

\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{i}-\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(\frac{1}{\underline{m}T}+\frac{1}{p^{2}})
,

(b)

\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{i}-\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}=\mathcal{O}_{p}((\frac{1}{\underline{m}T}+\frac{1}{p^{2}})^{1/2})
,

(c)

\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}=\mathcal{O}_{p}((\frac{1}{\underline{m}T}+\frac{1}{p^{2}})^{1/2})
.

Proof.

(a) First, we expand the last two terms as follows

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{i}-\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{i}-\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}})

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{l=1}^{p}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{i}-\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})^{\top}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{I}_{1}+\mathrm{I}_{2}.

We bound
\mathrm{I}_{1}
by properties of norms and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality as

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{I}_{1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{i}-\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{i}-\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{i}-\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}}\right\|_{2}.

By Proposition
1
and Theorem 1, we have

\left\|\mathrm{I}_{1}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\left(\frac{1}{\underline{m}T}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{3/2}\right).

Similarly, we bound
\mathrm{I}_{2}
as

\left\|\mathrm{I}_{2}\right\|_{2}\leq\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{i}-\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\frac{\left\|\mathbf{R}\right\|_{2}}{\sqrt{p}}\left\|\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}}\right\|_{2}.

By Assumption 3, Proposition
1
, and Lemma
1
, we have

\left\|\mathrm{I}_{2}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\underline{m}T}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right).

(b) First, we expand the last two terms as follows

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{i}-\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{i}-\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}})

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{i}-\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}_{1}+\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}_{2}.

We bound
\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}_{1}
by properties of norms and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality as

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}_{1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{i}-\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{i}-\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}\left\|\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}\left\|\mathbf{R}\right\|_{2}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{i}-\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\frac{\left\|\mathbf{R}\right\|_{2}}{\sqrt{p}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}}\right\|_{2}.

By Assumption 3, Propositions
1
-
2
and Theorem 1, we have

\left\|\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}_{1}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\underline{m}T}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right).

Similarly, we bound
\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}_{2}
as

\left\|\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}_{2}\right\|_{2}\leq\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{i}-\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\frac{\left\|\mathbf{R}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{p}\left\|\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}}\right\|_{2}.

By Assumption 3, Propositions
1
-
2
and Lemma
1
, we have

\left\|\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}_{2}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\left(\frac{1}{\underline{m}T}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{1/2}\right).

(c) First, we expand the last two terms as follows

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}})

\displaystyle\ \ \ +\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})^{\top}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}_{1}+\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}_{2}.

We bound
\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}_{1}
by properties of norms and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality as

\displaystyle\left\|\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}_{1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|\mathbf{R}^{i}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}})^{\top}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|\mathbf{R}^{i}\right\|_{2}\left\|\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathbf{R}^{i}\right\|_{2}\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}}\right\|_{2}.

By Assumption 3, Propositions
1
-
2
and Theorem 1, we have

\left\|\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}_{1}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\underline{m}T}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right).

Similarly, we bound
\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}_{2}
as

\left\|\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}_{2}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\mathbf{R}^{i}\right\|_{2}\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{1}}\left\|\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{j}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{j}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\frac{\left\|\mathbf{R}\right\|_{2}}{\sqrt{p}}\left\|\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}}\right\|_{2}.

By Assumption 3, Propositions
1
-
2
and Lemma
1
, we have

\left\|\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}\hskip-1.2pt\mathrm{I}_{2}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\left(\frac{1}{\underline{m}T}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\right)^{1/2}\right).

∎

Proposition 5
.

Under Assumptions 1-6 with known
k
and
r
, as
\underline{m},\underline{n},T\to\infty
, we have

\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}}{p}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}},\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{C}}{q}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{C}}.

Here the matrices
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
and
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{C}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
are given by
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}}=\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{R}}^{1/2}\Psi_{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{-1/2}
and
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{C}}^{1/2}\Psi_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{C}}}^{-1/2}
, where
\mathbf{V_{R}}
and
\mathbf{V_{C}}
are diagonal matrices of eigenvalues (ordered decreasingly) of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{1/2}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{R}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{1/2}
and
\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{C}}}^{1/2}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{C}}}^{1/2}
, respectively, with corresponding eigenvector matrices
\Psi_{\mathbf{R}}
and
\Psi_{\mathbf{C}}
satisfying
\Psi_{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\Psi_{\mathbf{R}}=\mathbf{I}
and
\Psi_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\Psi_{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{I}
, along with
\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}=\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1}(\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1})^{\top}
and
\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{C}}}=\frac{1}{s_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{2}}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{-1})^{\top}
.

Proof.

We only prove the conclusion for
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}
, since proof of results for
\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\mathbf{C}
is similar. From Equation (
A1
), we have

\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}}=\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}.

By left-multiplying
\frac{1}{p}(\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top})^{1/2}\mathbf{R}^{\top}
on the both sides and writing
\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{i}
as
\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{i}=\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{i}-{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{H}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}+{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{H}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}
, we obtain

{\mathbf{B}}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}}=({\mathbf{A}}+{\mathbf{d}}{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}){\mathbf{B}},

(A2)

where
k\times k
matrices
{\mathbf{A}}
,
{\mathbf{B}}
, and
{\mathbf{d}}
are defined as

{\mathbf{A}}=\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\right)^{1/2}\frac{\mathbf{R}^{\top}\mathbf{R}}{p}\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\right)^{1/2},

{\mathbf{B}}=\frac{1}{p}\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\right)^{1/2}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}},

and

\displaystyle{\mathbf{d}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\right)^{1/2}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\left[\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}})(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}})^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\ \ \ +\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}})\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}+\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}})^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\right)^{1/2}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\left(\mathrm{I}_{1}+\mathrm{I}_{2}+\mathrm{I}_{3}\right).

By Proposition
2
and Assumption 3, we have
\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}(\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top})^{1/2}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
. Following a similar argument to that in Theorem 1, term
\mathrm{I}_{1}
is dominated by
\mathrm{I}_{2}
and
\mathrm{I}_{3}
, with
\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\|\mathrm{I}_{2}\|_{2}=\mathbf{o}_{p}(1)
and
\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\|\mathrm{I}_{3}\|_{2}=\mathbf{o}_{p}(1)
. Together, we have

\|{\mathbf{d}}\|_{2}=\mathbf{o}_{p}(1).

By Proposition
4
, as
\underline{m},p,T\to\infty
, we have

{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}=\frac{1}{p}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\right)\frac{1}{p}\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\frac{1}{p}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\widehat{{\mathbf{M}}}_{\mathbf{R}}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}=\widehat{{\mathbf{V}}}_{\mathbf{R}}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{V_{R}}.

In other words, the eigenvalues of
{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}
are not close to zero nor infinity, and then
\|{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\|_{2}=\|{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}({\mathbf{B}}^{-1})^{\top}\|_{2}^{1/2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
.

Let
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{B}}}
be the diagonal matrix consisting of the diagonal elements of
{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}}
. As
\underline{m},p,T\to\infty
, we have
{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{B}}}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{V_{R}}
. Define the normalized
k\times k
matrix
{\mathbf{B}}^{\ast}={\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{B}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. Then,
\left\|{\mathbf{B}}^{\ast}\right\|_{2}=\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}({\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}^{\top}{\mathbf{B}})}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}1
. By Equation (
A2
), we have

{\mathbf{B}}^{\ast}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}}=({\mathbf{A}}+{\mathbf{d}}{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}){\mathbf{B}}^{\ast}.

Since
\|{\mathbf{d}}\|_{2}=\mathbf{o}_{p}(1)
and
\|{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
, by Assumption 3 and Proposition
3
(c), as
\underline{m},p,T\to\infty
, we have

{\mathbf{A}}+{\mathbf{d}}{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}={\mathbf{A}}+\mathbf{o}_{p}(1)\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{1/2}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{R}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{1/2}.

Since the eigenvalues of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{1/2}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{R}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{1/2}
are distinct under Assumption 6(b), the eigenvector perturbation theory (
17
,
17
, Section 6.12) implies the existence of a unique orthogonal eigenvector matrix
\Psi_{\mathbf{R}}
of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{1/2}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{R}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{1/2}
, such that

\left\|\Psi_{\mathbf{R}}-{\mathbf{B}}^{\ast}\right\|_{2}=\mathbf{o}_{p}(1).

Finally, we complete our proof as

\frac{\mathbf{R}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}}{p}=\left(\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\right)^{-1/2}{\mathbf{B}}^{\ast}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{B}}}^{1/2}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{-1/2}\Psi_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{R}}^{1/2}={\mathbf{Q}}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}.

∎

Proposition 6
.

Under Assumptions 1-6 with known
k
and
r
, as
\underline{m},\underline{n},T\to\infty
, we have

\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}}^{-1},\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{C}}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{C}}^{-1}.

Here the matrices
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
and
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{C}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
are given by
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}}=\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{R}}^{1/2}\Psi_{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{-1/2}
and
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{C}}^{1/2}\Psi_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{C}}}^{-1/2}
, where
\mathbf{V_{R}}
and
\mathbf{V_{C}}
are diagonal matrices of eigenvalues (ordered decreasingly) of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{1/2}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{R}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{1/2}
and
\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{C}}}^{1/2}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{C}}}^{1/2}
, respectively, with corresponding eigenvector matrices
\Psi_{\mathbf{R}}
and
\Psi_{\mathbf{C}}
satisfying
\Psi_{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\Psi_{\mathbf{R}}=\mathbf{I}
and
\Psi_{\mathbf{C}}^{\top}\Psi_{\mathbf{C}}=\mathbf{I}
, along with
\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}=\frac{1}{s_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1}(\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1})^{\top}
and
\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{C}}}=\frac{1}{s_{2}}\sum_{j=1}^{s_{2}}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{-1}(\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{-1})^{\top}
.

Proof.

We only prove the conclusion for
\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}}
, since proof of results for
\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{C}}
is similar.

By the definition of
{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathbf{R}}
, we have

{\mathbf{H}}_{\mathbf{R}}=\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}{\mathbf{H}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}{\mathbf{H}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}\widehat{{\mathbf{V}}}_{{\mathbf{R}}}^{-1}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{R}}^{-1}=\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{H_{R}}}^{1/2}\Psi_{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{R}}^{-1/2}=\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}}^{-1},

where the second step follows from Propositions
3
(c),
4
, and
5
, and the last step follows from
\Psi_{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\Psi_{\mathbf{R}}=\mathbf{I}
.
∎

The following lemma extends the asymptotic normality results in
8
to establish the joint asymptotic distribution of all rows of
\{\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\}_{i=1}^{s_{1}}
, which form the technical basis for the proof of Theorem 4.

Lemma 3
.

Let
\{\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}}}^{a,b}\}_{a,b\in[2]}
as in Theorem 4 for
i_{1},i_{2}\in[s_{1}]
and
j_{1},j_{2}\in[p]
, and
\{\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime},j_{1}^{\prime},j_{2}^{\prime}}}^{a,b}\}_{a,b\in[2]}
similarly for
i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime}\in[s_{2}]
and
j_{1}^{\prime},j_{2}^{\prime}\in[q]
. Under Assumptions 1-6 with known
k
and
r
, we have

(a)

When
{\sqrt{\overline{m}T}}/{p}=\mathbf{o}(1)
, for bounded matrices
\{{\mathbf{A}}^{i,j}\}_{i,j=1}^{s_{1},p}\subset\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
, any linear combination of the row vectors of
\{\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}-\mathbf{R}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\}_{i=1}^{s_{1}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sqrt{m_{i}T}{\mathbf{A}}^{i,j}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{j}_{i}-\mathbf{R}^{j}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\right)^{\top}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}{\mathbf{A}}^{i,j}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1}\frac{\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}}}{p}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{E}}}_{it}^{j})^{\top}+\mathbf{o}_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathcal{D}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{N}\left(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{R}},{\mathbf{A}}}\right),\quad\text{as }\underline{m},p,T\to\infty,

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{R}},{\mathbf{A}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p^{2}s_{1}^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{p}\sum_{j_{2}=1}^{p}{\mathbf{A}}^{i_{1},j_{1}}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1}}}^{-1}{\mathbf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1}}}\left(\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}}}^{1,1}+\alpha\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}}}^{1,2}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}^{\top}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.+\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}}}^{2,1}+\alpha^{2}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}}}^{2,2}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}^{\top}\right){\mathbf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{2}}}^{\top}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{2}}}^{-1}({\mathbf{A}}^{i_{2},j_{2}})^{\top}.

The matrix
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}=\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{1/2}\Psi_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FC}_{i}}^{-1/2}
, where
\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}
is the diagonal matrix of eigenvalues (ordered decreasingly) of
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FC}_{i}}^{1/2}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FC}_{i}}^{1/2}
, with the corresponding eigenvector matrix
\Psi_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}
satisfying
\Psi_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{\top}\Psi_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}=\mathbf{I}
, along with
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FC}_{i}}=\frac{1}{m_{i}}\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{t}\mathbf{C}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{C}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{t}^{\top}]
.

(b)

When
{\sqrt{\overline{n}T}}/{q}=\mathbf{o}(1)
, for bounded matrices
\{{\mathbf{B}}^{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}\}_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}=1}^{s_{2},q}\subset\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
, any linear combination of the row vectors of
\{\widehat{\mathbf{C}}_{i^{\prime}}-\mathbf{C}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{C}_{i^{\prime}}}\}_{i^{\prime}=1}^{s_{2}}
satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{qs_{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{s_{2}}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{q}\sqrt{n_{i^{\prime}}T}{\mathbf{B}}^{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{C}}^{j^{\prime}}_{i^{\prime}}-\mathbf{C}^{j^{\prime}}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{C}_{i^{\prime}}}\right)^{\top}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{qs_{2}}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{s_{2}}\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{q}{\mathbf{B}}^{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}_{i^{\prime}}}^{-1}\frac{\widehat{{\mathbf{C}}}_{i^{\prime}}^{\top}{\mathbf{C}}}{q}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{i^{\prime}}T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}}_{i^{\prime}}^{\top}[(\widetilde{\bm{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{i^{\prime}t}^{\top})^{j^{\prime}}]^{\top}+\mathbf{o}_{p}(1)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathcal{D}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{N}\left(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{B}}}\right),\quad\text{as }\underline{n},q,T\to\infty,

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{B}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{q^{2}s_{2}^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}^{\prime}=1}^{s_{2}}\sum_{i_{2}^{\prime}=1}^{s_{2}}\sum_{j_{1}^{\prime}=1}^{q}\sum_{j_{2}^{\prime}=1}^{q}{\mathbf{B}}^{i_{1}^{\prime},j_{1}^{\prime}}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}^{\prime}}}^{-1}{\mathbf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}^{\prime}}}\left(\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime},j_{1}^{\prime},j_{2}^{\prime}}}^{1,1}+\alpha\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime},j_{1}^{\prime},j_{2}^{\prime}}}^{1,2}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left.+\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}^{\top}\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime},j_{1}^{\prime},j_{2}^{\prime}}}^{2,1}+\alpha^{2}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}^{\top}\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}^{\prime},i_{2}^{\prime},j_{1}^{\prime},j_{2}^{\prime}}}^{2,2}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\right){\mathbf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{2}^{\prime}}}^{\top}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{2}^{\prime}}}^{-1}({\mathbf{B}}^{i_{2}^{\prime},j_{2}^{\prime}})^{\top}.

The matrix
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{C}_{i^{\prime}}}=\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{C}_{i^{\prime}}}^{1/2}\Psi_{\mathbf{C}_{i^{\prime}}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FR}_{i^{\prime}}}^{-1/2}
, where
\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{C}_{i^{\prime}}}
is the diagonal matrix of eigenvalues (ordered decreasingly) of
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FR}_{i^{\prime}}}^{1/2}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FR}_{i^{\prime}}}^{1/2}
, with the corresponding eigenvector matrix
\Psi_{\mathbf{C}_{i^{\prime}}}
satisfying
\Psi_{\mathbf{C}_{i^{\prime}}}^{\top}\Psi_{\mathbf{C}_{i^{\prime}}}=\mathbf{I}
, along with
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FR}_{i^{\prime}}}=\frac{1}{n_{i^{\prime}}}\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{t}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{i^{\prime}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{i^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathbf{F}}_{t}]
.

Proof.

We only prove (a), since the proof of (b) is similar. For each
i\in[s_{1}]
and
j\in[p]
, when
{\sqrt{m_{i}T}}/{p}=\mathbf{o}(1)
,
\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{j}_{i}-\mathbf{R}^{j}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}
can be replaced by its dominant term introduced in Proposition
3
(d). Specifically,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sqrt{m_{i}T}{\mathbf{A}}^{i,j}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{j}_{i}-\mathbf{R}^{j}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\right)^{\top}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}{\mathbf{A}}^{i,j}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1}\frac{\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}}}{p}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{E}}}_{it}^{j})^{\top}+\mathbf{o}_{p}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}{\mathbf{A}}^{i,j}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1}\frac{\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}}}{p}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}\frac{{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{E}}}_{it}^{j})^{\top}}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}+\mathbf{o}_{p}(1).

For convenience, let
\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i,j}={\mathbf{A}}^{i,j}\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1}{\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}}}/p\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k}
. As defined in Section 2.2,
\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}={\mathbf{F}}_{t}+\widetilde{\alpha}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}
,
\widetilde{{\mathbf{E}}}_{t}={\mathbf{E}}_{t}+\widetilde{\alpha}\overline{{\mathbf{E}}}
, and
\widetilde{\alpha}^{2}+2\widetilde{\alpha}=\alpha
. Then, we obtain the following decomposition,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i,j}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}(\widetilde{{\mathbf{E}}}_{it}^{j})^{\top}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i,j}\left({\mathbf{F}}_{t}+\widetilde{\alpha}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}\left[({\mathbf{E}}_{it}^{j})^{\top}+\widetilde{\alpha}(\overline{{\mathbf{E}}}_{i}^{j})^{\top}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i,j}\left[{\mathbf{F}}_{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{it}^{j})^{\top}+(\widetilde{\alpha}^{2}+2\widetilde{\alpha})\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}(\overline{{\mathbf{E}}}_{i}^{j})^{\top}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i,j}({\mathbf{F}}_{t}+\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}})\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{it}^{j})^{\top}\overset{\triangle}{=}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{x}}_{t},

where we define the
k\times 1
random vector
{\mathbf{x}}_{t}=\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i,j}({\mathbf{F}}_{t}+\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}})\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{it}^{j})^{\top}
. According to Proposition
3
(a)-(b), both
\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{-1}\|_{2}
and
\|{p}^{-1}{\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}}}\|_{2}
are bounded, implying that
\|\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i,j}\|_{2}
is bounded, for any
i\in[s_{1}]
and
j\in[p]
. By Assumptions 2(a),
\mathbb{E}\|{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}\|^{4}_{2}
is bounded, and hence
\|\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\|_{2}
is bounded by the ergodic theorem for
\alpha
-mixing processes in
13
. By Assumptions 3(a),
\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}\|{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\|_{2}
is also bounded. Consequently,
\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i,j}\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{it}^{j})^{\top}
inherits the
\alpha
-mixing property from vec(
{\mathbf{E}}_{t}
). Moreover, by Assumption 1, the process {vec(
{\mathbf{F}}_{t}
),vec(
{\mathbf{E}}_{t}
)} is
\alpha
-mixing, implying that
\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i,j}{\mathbf{F}}_{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{it}^{j})^{\top}
is also
\alpha
-mixing. Combining these two components, we conclude that
{\mathbf{x}}_{t}
is
\alpha
-mixing.

By Assumption 2,
\mathbb{E}[{\mathbf{E}}_{t}]=\textbf{0}
and
{\mathbf{E}}_{t}
is uncorrelated with
{\mathbf{F}}_{t}
, which implies that
\mathbb{E}[{\mathbf{x}}_{t}]=\textbf{0}
. In addition, for
m>2
given in Assumption 5, by the Holder’s inequality and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, for some
a,b
such that
1<a,b<\infty
and
1/a+1/b=1
, we obtain the following bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|{\mathbf{x}}_{t}\|^{m}_{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left\|\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i,j}({\mathbf{F}}_{t}+\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}})\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{it}^{j})^{\top}\right\|_{2}^{m}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\max_{i,j}\left\|\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i,j}\right\|_{2}^{m}\right)\left(\max_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left\|({\mathbf{F}}_{t}+\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}})\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{it}^{j})^{\top}\right\|_{2}^{m}\right)

\displaystyle\leq k^{m}\left(\max_{i,j}\left\|\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i,j}\right\|_{2}^{m}\right)\left(\max_{i,j,l}\mathbb{E}\left[({\mathbf{F}}_{t}^{l}+\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}^{l})\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{it}^{j})^{\top}\right]^{m}\right)

\displaystyle\leq k^{m}\left(\max_{i,j}\left\|\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i,j}\right\|_{2}^{m}\right)\left(\max_{i,j,l}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|{\mathbf{F}}_{t}^{l}+\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}^{l}\right\|_{2}^{m}\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{it}^{j})^{\top}\right\|_{2}^{m}\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq(1+|\alpha|)^{m}k^{m}\left(\max_{i,j}\left\|\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i,j}\right\|_{2}^{m}\right)\left[\max_{l}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\mathbf{F}}_{t}^{l}\|_{2}^{ma}\right]\right)^{1/a}\right]

\displaystyle\ \ \ \left[\max_{i,j}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{it}^{j})^{\top}\right\|_{2}^{mb}\right]\right)^{1/b}\right]

\displaystyle=(1+|\alpha|)^{m}k^{m}\left(\max_{i,j}\left\|\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i,j}\right\|_{2}^{m}\right)\left[\max_{l}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\mathbf{F}}_{t}^{l}\|_{2}^{ma}\right]\right)^{1/a}\right]

\displaystyle\ \ \ \left[\max_{i,j}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}\sum_{\tau=1}^{m_{i}}({\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\tau})^{\top}e_{it,j\tau}\right\|_{2}^{mb}\right]\right)^{1/b}\right].

Here
\{({\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\tau})^{\top}\}_{\tau=1}^{m_{i}}
are columns of
{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}
and
\{e_{it,j\tau}\}_{\tau=1}^{m_{i}}
are elements of
{\mathbf{E}}_{it}^{j}
, i.e,

{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}=\begin{pmatrix}({\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{1})^{\top}&({\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{2})^{\top}&...&({\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{m_{i}})^{\top}\end{pmatrix},

and

{\mathbf{E}}_{it}^{j}=\begin{pmatrix}e_{it,j1}&e_{it,j2}&...&e_{it,jm_{i}}\end{pmatrix}.

By Assumption 5(a), for any
l\in[k]
,
j\in[p]
, and
i\in[s_{1}]
, there exists
C>0
, s.t.,

\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\mathbf{F}}_{t}^{l}\|_{2}^{ma}\right]\leq C,\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}\sum_{\tau=1}^{m_{i}}({\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\tau})^{\top}e_{it,j\tau}\right\|_{2}^{mb}\right]=\mathcal{O}(1).

Thus, we have
\mathbb{E}[\|{\mathbf{x}}_{t}\|^{m}_{2}]<\infty
. Since
{\mathbf{x}}_{t}
is
\alpha
-mixing with
\mathbb{E}[{\mathbf{x}}_{t}]=\textbf{0}
, and
\mathbb{E}[\|{\mathbf{x}}_{t}\|^{m}_{2}]<\infty
for some
m>2
, the central limit theorem for
\alpha
-mixing processes (
13
,
13
, Theorem 2.21(i)) implies that, as
T\to\infty
,

\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}\overset{\mathcal{D}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{N}\left(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{R}},{\mathbf{A}}}^{p,q}\right),

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{R}},{\mathbf{A}}}^{p,q}

\displaystyle=\underset{T\rightarrow\infty}{\operatorname{plim}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\text{cov}({\mathbf{x}}_{t},{\mathbf{x}}_{s})

\displaystyle=\underset{T\rightarrow\infty}{\operatorname{plim}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{p}\frac{1}{p^{2}s_{1}^{2}}\begin{pmatrix}\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{1},j_{1}}&\alpha\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{1},j_{1}}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{F}}_{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i_{1}}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{{i_{1}}t}^{j_{1}})^{\top}\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i_{1}}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{{i_{1}}t}^{j_{1}})^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{F}}_{s}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i_{2}}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{2}}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{{i_{2}}s}^{j_{2}})^{\top}\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i_{2}}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{2}}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{{i_{2}}s}^{j_{2}})^{\top}\end{pmatrix}^{\top}\begin{pmatrix}(\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{2},j_{2}})^{\top}\\
\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}^{\top}(\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{2},j_{2}})^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p^{2}s_{1}^{2}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{p}\left[\underset{T\rightarrow\infty}{\operatorname{plim}}\begin{pmatrix}\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{1},j_{1}}&\alpha\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{1},j_{1}}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\end{pmatrix}\right]\left[\underset{T\rightarrow\infty}{\operatorname{plim}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t,s=1}^{T}\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{F}}_{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i_{1}}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{{i_{1}}t}^{j_{1}})^{\top}\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i_{1}}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{{i_{1}}t}^{j_{1}})^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{F}}_{s}\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i_{2}}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{2}}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{{i_{2}}s}^{j_{2}})^{\top}\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i_{2}}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i_{2}}^{\top}({\mathbf{E}}_{{i_{2}}s}^{j_{2}})^{\top}\end{pmatrix}^{\top}\right]\left[\underset{T\rightarrow\infty}{\operatorname{plim}}\begin{pmatrix}(\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{2},j_{2}})^{\top}\\
\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}^{\top}(\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{2},j_{2}})^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p^{2}s_{1}^{2}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{p}\left[\underset{T\rightarrow\infty}{\operatorname{plim}}\begin{pmatrix}\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{1},j_{1}}&\alpha\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{1},j_{1}}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\end{pmatrix}\right]\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}}}^{1,1}&\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}}}^{1,2}\\
\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}}}^{2,1}&\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}}}^{2,2}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left[\underset{T\rightarrow\infty}{\operatorname{plim}}\begin{pmatrix}(\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{2},j_{2}})^{\top}\\
\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}^{\top}(\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{2},j_{2}})^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\right].

By Proposition
3
(a)-(b), as
m_{i},p,T\to\infty
, we have

\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i,j}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{A}}^{i,j}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}^{-1}{\mathbf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}\overset{\triangle}{=}{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i,j}.

Then, as
\underline{m},p\to\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{R}},{\mathbf{A}}}^{p,q}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\frac{1}{p^{2}s_{1}^{2}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{p}\left[\underset{m_{i},p,T\rightarrow\infty}{\operatorname{plim}}\begin{pmatrix}\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{1},j_{1}}&\alpha\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{1},j_{1}}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\end{pmatrix}\right]\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}}}^{1,1}&\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}}}^{1,2}\\
\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}}}^{2,1}&\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}}}^{2,2}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \left[\underset{m_{i},p,T\rightarrow\infty}{\operatorname{plim}}\begin{pmatrix}(\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{2},j_{2}})^{\top}\\
\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}^{\top}(\widehat{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{2},j_{2}})^{\top}\end{pmatrix}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p^{2}s_{1}^{2}}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=1}^{p}\begin{pmatrix}{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{1},j_{1}}&\alpha{{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{1},j_{1}}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}}}^{1,1}&\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}}}^{1,2}\\
\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}}}^{2,1}&\mathbf{\Phi}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i_{1},i_{2},j_{1},j_{2}}}^{2,2}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \begin{pmatrix}({{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{2},j_{2}})^{\top}\\
\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}^{\top}({{\mathbf{U}}}^{i_{2},j_{2}})^{\top}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{R}},{\mathbf{A}}}.

In other words, the final results are derived. As
\underline{m},p,T\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{ps_{1}}\sum_{i=1}^{s_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\sqrt{m_{i}T}{\mathbf{A}}^{i,j}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{R}}^{j}_{i}-\mathbf{R}^{j}\mathbf{H}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\right)^{\top}\overset{\mathcal{D}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{N}\left(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{\Sigma}_{{\mathbf{R}},{\mathbf{A}}}\right).

∎

A.2 Propositions and lemmas for the unit-root nonstationary case

We next present some propositions and lemmas for the nonstationary case. These results will be used to prove Theorems 6-8. We begin by establishing asymptotic properties of the local estimators and the associated auxiliary matrices. Following Lemma B.3 in
31
, we have Proposition
7
.

Proposition 7
.

Under Assumptions 3-4 and 7-9 with known
k
and
r
, for any
i\in[s_{1}]\text{ and }i^{\prime}\in[s_{2}]
, we have

\left\|\frac{1}{m_{i}T^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}^{\top}-\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FC}_{i}}^{*}\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}=\mathbf{o}_{p}\left(1\right),

and

\left\|\frac{1}{n_{j}T^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}}_{j}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}-\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FR}_{j}}^{*}\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}=\mathbf{o}_{p}\left(1\right).

The matrices
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FC}_{i}}^{*}=\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{{\mathbf{W}}}(u)\bm{\Omega}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{i}}\widetilde{{\mathbf{W}}}(u)^{\top}\text{d}u
and
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FR}_{j}}^{*}=\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{{\mathbf{W}}}(u)^{\top}\bm{\Omega}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{j}}\widetilde{{\mathbf{W}}}(u)\text{d}u
, where
\widetilde{{\mathbf{W}}}(u)={\mathbf{W}}(u)+\frac{\widetilde{\alpha}}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{W}}(t/T)
and
{\mathbf{W}}(\cdot)
is an
k\times r
matrix of Brownian motions with the covariance of
\text{vec}({\mathbf{W}}(\cdot))
being
\overline{{\mathbf{G}}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{u}}\overline{{\mathbf{G}}}^{\top}
.

Proof.

We only prove the conclusion for
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}^{\top}
, since the proof of the results for
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}}_{j}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}}_{j}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}
is similar. As defined in Section 2.2,
\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}={\mathbf{F}}_{t}+\widetilde{\alpha}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}
. Then, we obtain the following decomposition,

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}^{\top}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left({{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}+\widetilde{\alpha}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\right){\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\left({{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}+\widetilde{\alpha}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\right)^{\top}.

Following Lemma B.3 in
31
,

\max_{t\in[T]}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|{\mathbf{F}}_{t}-{\mathbf{W}}(t/T)\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}=\mathbf{o}_{p}\left(1\right).

Then,

\displaystyle\left\|\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{W}}(t/T)\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}-{\mathbf{W}}(t/T)\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\|{\mathbf{F}}_{t}-{\mathbf{W}}(t/T)\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{o}_{p}\left(1\right).

Combine two parts together, we have

\max_{t\in[T]}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}-\widetilde{{\mathbf{W}}}(t/T)\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}=\mathbf{o}_{p}\left(1\right).

By Assumption 3 and the continuous mapping theorem, the final results are derived.
∎

Proposition 8
.

Under Assumptions 3-4 and 7-8 with known
k
and
r
, for any
i\in[s_{1}]\text{ and }i^{\prime}\in[s_{2}]
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}\widetilde{{\mathbf{E}}}_{it}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}\right]=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(pm_{i}T^{2}\right),\quad\text{as }m_{i},p,T\to\infty,

and

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}}_{i^{\prime}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{i^{\prime}t}\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}\right]=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(qn_{i^{\prime}}T^{2}\right),\quad\text{as }n_{i^{\prime}},q,T\to\infty.

Proof.

We only prove the conclusion for
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}\widetilde{{\mathbf{E}}}_{it}^{\top}
, since the proof of the results for
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}^{\top}{\mathbf{R}}_{i^{\prime}}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{{{\varepsilon}}}}_{i^{\prime}t}
is similar. As defined in Section 2.2,
\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}={\mathbf{F}}_{t}+\widetilde{\alpha}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}
,
\widetilde{{\mathbf{E}}}_{t}={\mathbf{E}}_{t}+\widetilde{\alpha}\overline{{\mathbf{E}}}
, and
\widetilde{\alpha}^{2}+2\widetilde{\alpha}=\alpha
. Then, we obtain the following decomposition,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}\widetilde{{\mathbf{E}}}_{it}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left({{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}+\widetilde{\alpha}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}\right){\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}\left({{\mathbf{E}}}_{it}^{\top}+\widetilde{\alpha}\overline{{{\mathbf{E}}}}_{i}^{\top}\right)\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}{{\mathbf{E}}}_{it}^{\top}+(2\widetilde{\alpha}+\widetilde{\alpha}^{2})\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}{{{\mathbf{E}}}}_{it}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}({{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}+\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}){\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}{{\mathbf{E}}}_{it}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}({{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}+\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}){\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}({{\mathbf{E}}}_{it}^{j})^{\top}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}({{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}+\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}})\Big[\sum_{l=1}^{m_{i}}({\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{l})^{\top}e_{it,jl}\Big]\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Let
{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{ij}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{i}}}\sum_{l=1}^{m_{i}}({\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{l})^{\top}e_{it,jl}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times 1}
. By Assumptions 7-8, following Lemma B.2. in
31
, we have

\frac{1}{T^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{ij}\right\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}(1).

Additionally, by Assumption 8, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T^{2}}\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{ij}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T^{3}}\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}{{\mathbf{F}}}_{s}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{ij}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T^{3}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\sum_{s=1}^{T}{\mathbf{F}}_{s}\right\|_{2}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{ij}\right\|_{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T^{3}}\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{s=1}^{T}{\mathbf{F}}_{s}\right\|_{2}\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{ij}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}\max_{s\in[T]}\mathbb{E}\left\|{\mathbf{F}}_{s}\right\|_{2}\max_{t\in[T]}\mathbb{E}\left\|{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{ij}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(1),

where
\max_{t\in[T]}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|{\mathbf{F}}_{t}-{\mathbf{W}}(t/T)\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}=\mathbf{o}_{p}\left(1\right)
by Lemma B.3 in
31
. Together, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}\widetilde{{\mathbf{E}}}_{it}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathsf{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}({{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}+\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}})\Big[\sum_{l=1}^{m_{i}}({\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{l})^{\top}e_{it,jl}\Big]\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=m_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}({{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}+\alpha\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}){\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{ij}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq m_{i}\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{ij}\right\|_{2}^{2}+m_{i}\alpha\sum_{j=1}^{p}\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\overline{{\mathbf{F}}}{\mathbf{x}}_{t}^{ij}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(pm_{i}T^{2}\right).

∎

Proposition 9
.

Under Assumptions 3-4 and 7-9 with known
k
and
r
, for any
i\in[s_{1}]
and
j\in[s_{2}]
, as
m_{i},n_{j},T\to\infty
, we have

\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{*}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}^{*}\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{*}\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{V}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{j}}^{*},

with
\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{*}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
,
\|(\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{*})^{-1}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
,
\|\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{*}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
, and
\|(\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{*})^{-1}\|_{2}=\mathcal{O}_{p}(1)
. Here
\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{*}
and
\mathbf{V}_{\mathbf{C}_{j}}^{*}
are diagonal matrices of eigenvalues (ordered decreasingly) of
(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FC}_{i}}^{*})^{1/2}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{R}}(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FC}_{i}}^{*})^{1/2}
and
(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FR}_{j}}^{*})^{1/2}\bm{\Omega}_{\mathbf{C}}(\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FR}_{j}}^{*})^{1/2}
, respectively. The matrices
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FC}_{i}}^{*}=\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{{\mathbf{W}}}(u)\bm{\Omega}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{i}}\widetilde{{\mathbf{W}}}(u)^{\top}\text{d}u
and
\widetilde{\bm{\Sigma}}_{\mathbf{FR}_{j}}^{*}=\int_{0}^{1}\widetilde{{\mathbf{W}}}(u)^{\top}\bm{\Omega}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{j}}\widetilde{{\mathbf{W}}}(u)\text{d}u
, where
\widetilde{{\mathbf{W}}}(u)={\mathbf{W}}(u)+\frac{\widetilde{\alpha}}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbf{W}}(t/T)
and
{\mathbf{W}}(\cdot)
is an
k\times r
matrix of Brownian motions with the covariance of
\text{vec}({\mathbf{W}}(\cdot))
being
\overline{{\mathbf{G}}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\mathbf{u}}\overline{{\mathbf{G}}}^{\top}
.

Proof.

We only prove the conclusion for
\widehat{{\mathbf{V}}}_{{\mathbf{R}}_{i}}^{*}
, since the proof of results for
\widehat{{\mathbf{V}}}_{{\mathbf{C}}_{j}}^{*}
is similar. According to Equation (2.5) and
\frac{1}{p}\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{{\mathbf{R}}}_{i}={\mathbf{I}}
, we have

\widehat{\mathbf{V}}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}^{*}=\frac{1}{pT}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{M}}_{\mathbf{R}_{i}}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}=\frac{1}{p}\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}^{\top}\left(\frac{1}{pm_{i}T^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{it}\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{it}^{\top}\right)\widehat{\mathbf{R}}_{i}.

(A3)

According to model (2.4),
\widetilde{{\mathbf{Y}}}_{it}={\mathbf{R}}\widetilde{{\mathbf{F}}}_{t}{\mathbf{C}}_{i}^{\top}+\widetilde{{\mathbf{E}}}_{it}^{\top}
, we obtain
